Next pre-pass source facts gathered: Robots.bas 1524-1560 visits each robot for upkeep, poison, ManageFixed, CalcMass, obstacles/border, TieHooke/TieTorque, NetForces, BucketsCollision, static force filtering and tie data. ManageFixed uses mem216 > 0, so a negative fixpos is not fixed, and a later tie write must wait until the next ManageFixed visit. Physics.TieHooke 465-560 applies spring force only to the current endpoint; later endpoint calls can see earlier collision changes. PlanetEaters 575-607 visits later slots and adds equal/opposite FORCE to both; fixed actors do not invoke NetForces. Quads.BucketsCollision 223-265 checks only robnumber > n, within current bucket then eight adjacent buckets. SpatialIndex currently has only bulk rebuild and stores dense cells/entry-to-slot vectors; native sequential collision will need efficient position updates (a local sparse grid or a focused move-slot method), not an O(N) rebuild per bot. Native physics must preserve AddedMass sampled before the pre-pass, because corpse decay later changes radius. Current native_intent_phase allocates whole-world positions/masses/planet arrays; do not call it once per actor without refactoring to avoid O(N squared) work. Source fixes still unimplemented.

Corpse transitions/materialization clear the latch; restriction updates invalidate it. Tests for negative fixpos/later writes and radius-change added-mass caching are written, UNRUN. Important correction to the proposed grid: original Quads.BucketSize is 4000 (not the port's 1000 sensing cells). Source checks current bucket first, then left, right, top, bottom, left/up, left/down, right/up, right/down. Original bucket rows preserve insertion order; Delete_Bot slides entries, Add_Bot appends into the first empty slot. UpdateBotBucket clamps to floor(world_size/4000)-1. Physics.Repel3 does not call UpdateBotBucket; bucket membership can remain stale after collision until UpdatePosition. A faster fine grid may filter actual contacts, but must preserve this coarse bucket/order behavior for multi-contact native comparisons. Do not assume all pair order is ascending globally.
